Sum of weights\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-q7-sum-of-weights\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Q7. Sum of weights\" href=\"#q7-sum-of-weights\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003col dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eSelect all the cars from Asia\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eSelect only columns \u003ccode\u003evehicle_weight\u003c/code\u003e and \u003ccode\u003emodel_year\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eSelect the first 7 values\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eGet the underlying NumPy array. Let's call it \u003ccode\u003eX\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eCompute matrix-matrix multiplication between the transpose of \u003ccode\u003eX\u003c/code\u003e and \u003ccode\u003eX\u003c/code\u003e. To get the transpose, use \u003ccode\u003eX.T\u003c/code\u003e. Let's call the result \u003ccode\u003eXTX\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eInvert \u003ccode\u003eXTX\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eCreate an array \u003ccode\u003ey\u003c/code\u003e with values \u003ccode\u003e[1100, 1300, 800, 900, 1000, 1100, 1200]\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eMultiply the inverse of \u003ccode\u003eXTX\u003c/code\u003e with the transpose of \u003ccode\u003eX\u003c/code\u003e, and then multiply the result by \u003ccode\u003ey\u003c/code\u003e. Call the result \u003ccode\u003ew\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eWhat's the sum of all the elements of the result?\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ol\u003e\n\u003cblockquote\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eNote\u003c/strong\u003e: You just implemented linear regression. We'll talk about it in the next lesson.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/blockquote\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e0.0369\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e0.369\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e3.69\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e36.9\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eLearning in Public\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-learning-in-public\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Learning in Public\" href=\"#learning-in-public\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAs part of this course, we encourage you to learn in public. Homework 1","","\u003e [!NOTE]","\u003e This homework uses the pinned 2026 car fuel-efficiency release in the course","\u003e repository. The plan and report are available in `cohorts/2026/data/`.","## Set up the environment","","You need to install Python, NumPy, Pandas, Matplotlib, and Seaborn. For that, you can use the instructions from","[06-environment.md](../../../../01-intro/06-environment.md).","","## Q1. Pandas version","","What version of Pandas did you install?","","You can get the version information using the `__version__` field:","","```python","pd.__version__","```","","## Getting the data ","","For this homework, we'll use the 2026 Car Fuel Efficiency dataset. Download it from \u003ca href='https://raw.githubusercontent.com/DataTalksClub/machine-learning-zoomcamp/main/cohorts/2026/data/car_fuel_efficiency_2026.csv'\u003ehere\u003c/a\u003e.","","You can do it with wget:","```bash","wget https://raw.githubusercontent.com/DataTalksClub/machine-learning-zoomcamp/main/cohorts/2026/data/car_fuel_efficiency_2026.csv","```","","Or just open it with your browser and click \"Save as...\".","","Now read it with Pandas.","","## Q2. Records count","","How many records are in the dataset?","","- 5000","- 9000","- 10000","- 15000","","## Q3. Fuel types","","How many fuel types are presented in the dataset?","","- 1","- 2","- 3","- 4","","## Q4. Missing values","","How many columns in the dataset have missing values?","","- 0","- 1","- 2","- 3","- 4","","## Q5. Max fuel efficiency","","What's the maximum fuel efficiency of cars from Asia?","","- 21.2","- 31.2","- 41.2","- 51.2","","## Q6. Median value of horsepower","","1. Find the median value of the `horsepower` column in the dataset.","2. Next, calculate the most frequent value of the same `horsepower` column.","3. Use the `fillna` method to fill the missing values in the `horsepower` column with the most frequent value from the previous step.","4. Now, calculate the median value of `horsepower` once again.","","Has it changed?","","","- Yes, it increased","- Yes, it decreased","- No","","","## Q7. Sum of weights","","1. Select all the cars from Asia","2. Select only columns `vehicle_weight` and `model_year`","3. Select the first 7 values","4. Get the underlying NumPy array. Let's call it `X`.","5. Compute matrix-matrix multiplication between the transpose of `X` and `X`. To get the transpose, use `X.T`. Let's call the result `XTX`.","6. Invert `XTX`.","7. Create an array `y` with values `[1100, 1300, 800, 900, 1000, 1100, 1200]`.","8. Multiply the inverse of `XTX` with the transpose of `X`, and then multiply the result by `y`. Call the result `w`.","9. What's the sum of all the elements of the result?","","\u003e **Note**: You just implemented linear regression. We'll talk about it in the next lesson.","","- 0.0369","- 0.369","- 3.69","- 36.9","","## Learning in Public","","As part of this course, we encourage you to learn in public. Sharing your progress helps you reflect, stay accountable, and connect with others. Read the [learning in public guide](https://datatalks.club/docs/courses/zoomcamp-logistics/learning-in-public/) for more examples and submission guidance. After publishing, submit the post link with your homework.","","Write posts on your social media about Module 1 of this course.","","How to do it","- Pick one platform (or more!) and post using the templates below","- Don't forget to tag Alexey Grigorev and use #mlzoomcamp hashtag","","Alexey on social media: [LinkedIn](https://www.linkedin.com/in/agrigorev/), [Twitter/X](https://x.com/Al_Grigor)","DTC on social media: [LinkedIn](https://www.linkedin.com/company/datatalks-club/), [Twitter/X](https://x.com/DataTalksClub)","","---","","### LinkedIn aria-label="Permalink: Homework 1" href="#homework-1"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path <div class="markdown-alert markdown-alert-note" dir="auto"><p class="markdown-alert-title" dir="auto"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-info mr-2" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="M0 8a8 8 0 1 1 16 0A8 8 0 0 1 0 8Zm8-6.5a6.5 6.5 0 1 0 0 13 6.5 6.5 0 0 0 0-13ZM6.5 7.75A.75.75 0 0 1 7.25 7h1a.75.75 0 0 1 .75.75v2.75h.25a.75.75 0 0 1 0 1.5h-2a.75.75 0 0 1 0-1.5h.25v-2h-.25a.75.75 0 0 1-.75-.75ZM8 6a1 1 0 1 1 0-2 1 1 0 0 1 0 2Z"></path></svg>Note</p><p dir="auto">This homework uses the pinned 2026 car fuel-efficiency release in the course
repository. The plan and report are available in <code>cohorts/2026/data/</code>.</p>
</div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Set up the environment</h2><a id="user-content-set-up-the-environment" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Set up the environment" href="#set-up-the-environment"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">You need to install Python, NumPy, Pandas, Matplotlib, and Seaborn. For that, you can use the instructions from
<a href="/DataTalksClub/machine-learning-zoomcamp/blob/main/01-intro/06-environment.md">06-environment.md</a>.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Q1. Pandas version</h2><a id="user-content-q1-pandas-version" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Q1. Pandas version" href="#q1-pandas-version"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">What version of Pandas did you install?</p>
<p dir="auto">You can get the version information using the <code>__version__</code> field:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="pd.__version__"><pre><span class="pl-s1">pd</span>.<span class="pl-c1">__version__</span></pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Getting the data</h2><a id="user-content-getting-the-data" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Getting the data" href="#getting-the-data"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">For this homework, we'll use the 2026 Car Fuel Efficiency dataset. Download it from <a href="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/DataTalksClub/machine-learning-zoomcamp/main/cohorts/2026/data/car_fuel_efficiency_2026.csv" rel="nofollow">here</a>.</p>
<p dir="auto">You can do it with wget:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="wget https://raw.githubusercontent.com/DataTalksClub/machine-learning-zoomcamp/main/cohorts/2026/data/car_fuel_efficiency_2026.csv"><pre>wget https://raw.githubusercontent.com/DataTalksClub/machine-learning-zoomcamp/main/cohorts/2026/data/car_fuel_efficiency_2026.csv</pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Or just open it with your browser and click "Save as...".</p>
<p dir="auto">Now read it with Pandas.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Q2. Records count</h2><a id="user-content-q2-records-count" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Q2. <ol dir="auto">
<li>Find the median value of the <code>horsepower</code> column in the dataset.</li>
<li>Next, calculate the most frequent value of the same <code>horsepower</code> column.</li>
<li>Use the <code>fillna</code> method to fill the missing values in the <code>horsepower</code> column with the most frequent value from the previous step.</li>
<li>Now, calculate the median value of <code>horsepower</code> once again.</li>
</ol>
<p dir="auto">Has it changed?</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>Yes, it increased</li>
<li>Yes, it decreased</li>
<li>No</li>
</ul>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Q7. Sum of weights</h2><a id="user-content-q7-sum-of-weights" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Q7. Sum of weights" href="#q7-sum-of-weights"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<ol dir="auto">
<li>Select all the cars from Asia</li>
<li>Select only columns <code>vehicle_weight</code> and <code>model_year</code></li>
<li>Select the first 7 values</li>
<li>Get the underlying NumPy array. Let's call it <code>X</code>.</li>
<li>Compute matrix-matrix multiplication between the transpose of <code>X</code> and <code>X</code>. To get the transpose, use <code>X.T</code>. Let's call the result <code>XTX</code>.</li>
<li>Invert <code>XTX</code>.</li>
<li>Create an array <code>y</code> with values <code>[1100, 1300, 800, 900, 1000, 1100, 1200]</code>.</li>
<li>Multiply the inverse of <code>XTX</code> with the transpose of <code>X</code>, and then multiply the result by <code>y</code>. Call the result <code>w</code>.</li>
<li>What's the sum of all the elements of the result?</li>
</ol>
<blockquote>
<p dir="auto"><strong>Note</strong>: You just implemented linear regression. We'll talk about it in the next lesson.</p>
</blockquote>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>0.0369</li>
<li>0.369</li>
<li>3.69</li>
<li>36.9</li>
</ul>
